\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|N^{(n)}\right\|_{C^{0}_{\varepsilon}}\left(\left\|\tau^{(n)}\partial_{t}\tau^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta+2+\varepsilon}}+\left\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\gamma}^{(n)}(\tau^{(n)})^{2}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta+2+\varepsilon}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left|\partial_{t}N_{a}^{(n)}\right|\left\|(\tau^{(n)})^{2}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta+2+3\varepsilon}}+\left\|\partial_{t}\tilde{N}^{(n)}(\tau^{(n)})^{2}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta+2+2\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}C(A_{3})C_{i}.

where in the last inequality we use
\left\|N^{(n)}\right\|_{C^{0}_{\varepsilon}}\lesssim 1
(which comes from (
5.8
)),
\left\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\gamma}^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\lesssim C_{i}
(wich comes from (
5.1
)), (
5.9
) and the product estimate together with (
5.32
) (for
(\tau^{(n)})^{2}
). For
\tau^{(n)}\partial_{t}\tau^{(n)}
, we use the Hölder’s inequality (
L^{4}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}\times L^{4}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}\xhookrightarrow{}L^{2}_{\delta+2+\varepsilon}
), the embedding
H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}\xhookrightarrow{}L^{4}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}
, (
5.32
) and (
5.22
).

We now turn to the compactly supported term
\partial_{t}\left(\frac{e^{2\gamma^{(n)}}}{N^{(n)}}\left(e_{0}^{(n-1)}\varphi^{(n)}\right)^{2}\right)
. We use (
4.2
) for the
e^{2\gamma^{(n)}}
factor and
\left|\frac{1}{N^{(n)}}\right|+\left\|\chi\ln\right\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{2R})}\lesssim 1
:

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{t}\left(\frac{e^{2\gamma^{(n)}}}{N^{(n)}}\left(e_{0}^{(n-1)}\varphi^{(n)}\right)^{2}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|e_{0}^{(n-1)}\varphi^{(n)}\partial_{t}\left(e_{0}^{(n-1)}\varphi^{(n)}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\gamma}^{(n)}\left(e_{0}^{(n-1)}\varphi^{(n)}\right)^{2}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\|\partial_{t}\tilde{N}^{(n)}\left(e_{0}^{(n-1)}\varphi^{(n)}\right)^{2}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left|\partial_{t}N_{a}^{(n)}\right|\left\|\left(e_{0}^{(n-1)}\varphi^{(n)}\right)^{2}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|e_{0}^{(n-1)}\varphi^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{4}}\left(C_{i}\left\|e_{0}^{(n-1)}\varphi^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{4}}+\left\|\partial_{t}\left(e_{0}^{(n-1)}\varphi^{(n)}\right)\right\|_{H^{1}}\right)\lesssim\varepsilon C_{i},

where in the last inequality we use
\left\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\gamma}^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\lesssim C_{i}
, (
5.9
) and the embedding
L^{4}\times H^{1}\xhookrightarrow{}L^{2}
. The term
\partial_{t}\left(\frac{e^{2\gamma^{(n)}-4\varphi^{(n)}}}{N^{(n)}}\left(e_{0}^{(n-1)}\omega^{(n)}\right)^{2}\right)
is handled in the same way, using (
4.3
) and (
5.28
) to get rid of the
e^{-4\varphi^{(n)}}
. Combining all these estimates concludes the proof of (
5.35
).

We now turn to the proof of (
5.36
). To obtain the
H^{4}_{\delta}
bound for
\tilde{N}^{(n+1)}
, we need to control the RHS of (
5.1
) in
H^{2}_{\delta+2}
. Since we already know that the RHS of (
5.1
) is in
H^{1}_{\delta+2}
with bound
\varepsilon C(A_{1})C_{i}
, it remains to bound the
L^{2}_{\delta+4}
norm of the second derivative the RHS of (
5.1
) :

•

for the term
e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}N^{(n)}|H^{(n)}|^{2}
, we first use (
4.1
), and then the embedding
H^{1}_{\delta+1}\xhookrightarrow{}L^{4}_{\delta+1}
, (
5.16
), (
5.29
), (
5.8
), (
5.9
) and (
5.10
) :

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{2}\left(e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}N^{(n)}|H^{(n)}|^{2}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta+4}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla^{2}N^{(n)}(H^{(n)})^{2}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta+4}}+\left\|\nabla N^{(n)}H^{(n)}\nabla H^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta+4}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\|N^{(n)}H^{(n)}\nabla^{2}H^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta+4}}+\left\|N^{(n)}(\nabla H^{(n)})^{2}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta+4}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}C(A_{2})C_{i}^{2}

where we used
L^{\infty}
bounds for
N^{(n)}
,
\nabla N^{(n)}
and
\nabla^{2}N^{(n)}
(see (
5.8
), (
5.9
) and (
5.10
) respectively). Note that for
N
the logarithmic growth is handled by adding a small weight. We also used (
5.16
) and (
5.29
) and the product law to handle the
H^{(n)}
terms.

•

for the term
e^{2\gamma^{(n)}}N^{(n)}(\tau^{(n)})^{2}
, we note that
\tau^{(n)}
and
H^{(n)}
satisfy the exact same estimate (according to (
5.16
), (
5.20
)), except for a slight difference of weights (
\delta^{\prime}
instead of
\delta
) and constants (
A_{1}
compared to 2). Therefore we treat this term exactly as the previous one and omit the details.

•

we next discuss the compactly supported term
\frac{e^{2\gamma^{(n)}}}{N^{(n)}}\left(e_{0}^{(n-1)}\varphi^{(n)}\right)^{2}
. We first use (
4.1
) :

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{2}\left(\frac{e^{2\gamma^{(n)}}}{N^{(n)}}\left(e_{0}^{(n-1)}\varphi^{(n)}\right)^{2}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|e_{0}^{(n-1)}\varphi^{(n)}\nabla^{2}\left(e_{0}^{(n-1)}\varphi^{(n)}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\left(\nabla\left(e_{0}^{(n-1)}\varphi^{(n)}\right)\right)^{2}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\left\|\nabla N^{(n)}e_{0}^{(n-1)}\varphi^{(n)}\nabla\left(e_{0}^{(n-1)}\varphi^{(n)}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla^{2}N^{(n)}\left(e_{0}^{(n-1)}\varphi^{(n)}\right)^{2}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\left\|\left(\nabla N^{(n)}\right)^{2}\left(e_{0}^{(n-1)}\varphi^{(n)}\right)^{2}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}C(A_{2})C_{i}^{2}+C(A_{0})C_{i}^{2},

where we used (
5.9
), (
5.26
) and (
5.28
). The idea is to use
L^{\infty}
-bounds for
\nabla N^{(n)}
and
\nabla^{2}N^{(n)}
and the Hölder’s inequality to deal with the product of terms depending on
\varphi^{(n)}
.

•

the term
\frac{e^{2\gamma^{(n)}-4\varphi^{(n)}}}{N^{(n)}}\left(e_{0}^{(n-1)}\omega^{(n)}\right)^{2}
is handled in a similar way, but we have to be careful about the case where two derivatives hit
e^{-4\varphi^{(n)}}
. Using (
4.3
), (
4.2
) and
1\lesssim N^{(n)}
, this leads to estimating the following term :

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{2}\varphi^{(n)}\left(e_{0}^{(n-1)}\omega^{(n)}\right)^{2}\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\left\|\nabla^{2}\varphi^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|e_{0}^{(n-1)}\omega^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|e_{0}^{(n-1)}\omega^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\varepsilon C(A_{0})C_{i}^{2},

where we used (
5.26
), (
5.27
) and (
5.28
).

This concludes the proof of (
5.36
).
∎

The following lemma will allow us to estimate the
H^{1}
norm of solutions of elliptic equations.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
\xi=(\xi^{1},\xi^{2})
a vector field on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, for all
\sigma<1
the following holds :

(5.37)

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\xi\right\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}\lesssim\left\|L\xi\right\|_{L^{2}_{1}}.

Proof.

We set
A_{ij}\vcentcolon=(L\xi)_{ij}
and take the divergence to obtain
\Delta\xi^{i}=\delta^{ij}\partial^{k}A_{kj}
. Let
w(x)=\langle x\rangle^{2\sigma}
. We multiply this equation by
w\xi^{\ell}
, contract it with
\delta_{i\ell}
and integrate over
\mathbb{R}^{2}
to get (after integrating by parts) :

\delta_{i\ell}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla(w\xi^{\ell})\cdot\nabla\xi^{i}\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\partial^{k}(w\xi^{\ell})A_{k\ell}\mathrm{d}x,

which becomes

\left\|\nabla\xi\right\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Delta w|\xi|^{2}\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}w\partial^{k}\xi^{\ell}A_{k\ell}\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\partial^{k}w\xi^{\ell}A_{k\ell}\mathrm{d}x.

Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the trick
ab\leq\eta a^{2}+\frac{1}{\eta}b^{2}
, we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}w\partial^{k}\xi^{\ell}A_{k\ell}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\left\|\nabla\xi\right\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}\left\|A\right\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}\lesssim\eta\left\|\nabla\xi\right\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}^{2}+\frac{1}{\eta}\left\|A\right\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}^{2}.

We note that
|\nabla w|\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{2\sigma-1}
and
|\Delta w|\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{2\sigma-2}
, which imply that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\partial^{k}w\xi^{\ell}A_{k\ell}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\left\|\xi\right\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma-1}}\left\|A\right\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}\lesssim\left\|\xi\right\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma-1}}^{2}+\left\|A\right\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Delta w|\xi|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\left\|\xi\right\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma-1}}^{2}.

Thus,

\left\|\nabla\xi\right\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}^{2}\lesssim\left\|\xi\right\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma-1}}^{2}+\left(1+\frac{1}{\eta}\right)\left\|A\right\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}^{2}+\eta\left\|\nabla\xi\right\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}^{2}.

We take
\eta
small enough in order to absorb
\eta\left\|\nabla\xi\right\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}^{2}
into the LHS. Taking the square root of the inequality we obtained, we get :

\left\|\nabla\xi\right\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}\lesssim\left\|\xi\right\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma-1}}+\left\|A\right\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}.

It remains to show that
\left\|\xi\right\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma-1}}\lesssim\left\|A\right\|_{L^{2}_{1}}
. For that, we start by using Lemma
B.2
:
\sigma<1
so there exists
r>2
such that
\sigma<\frac{2}{r}<1
. According to Lemma
B.2
, we have
\left\|\xi\right\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma-1}}\lesssim\left\|\xi\right\|_{L^{r}}
. Recalling that
\Delta\xi^{i}=\delta^{ij}\partial^{k}A_{kj}
we have :

\xi^{i}(x)=\frac{\delta^{ij}}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\ln|x-y|\partial^{k}A_{kj}\mathrm{d}y=-\frac{\delta^{ij}}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{y^{k}-x^{k}}{|x-y|^{2}}A_{kj}\mathrm{d}y.\

Therefore we can use the Hardy-Littlewood-Sobolev inequality (Proposition
B.4
), that

(5.38)

\left\|\xi\right\|_{L^{r}}\lesssim\left\|A*\frac{1}{|\cdot|}\right\|_{L^{r}}\lesssim\left\|A\right\|_{L^{\frac{2r}{2+r}}}.

We again use Lemma
B.2
to get the embedding
L^{2}_{1}\xhookrightarrow{}L^{\frac{2r}{2+r}}
(recall that
r>2
), which conclude the proof of (
5.37
).

∎

Proposition 5.4
.

For
n\geq 2
, the following estimates hold :

(5.39)

\displaystyle\left\|\beta^{(n+1)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{2},

(5.40)

\displaystyle\left\|\beta^{(n+1)}\right\|_{H^{3}_{\delta^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{i},

(5.41)

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla e_{0}^{(n)}\beta^{(n+1)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon C_{i},

(5.42)

\displaystyle\left\|e_{0}^{(n)}\beta^{(n+1)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim A_{0}C_{i},

(5.43)

\displaystyle\left\|e_{0}^{(n)}\beta^{(n+1)}\right\|_{H^{3}_{\delta^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim A_{3}C_{i}^{2}.

Proof.

In view of Proposition
5.2
and (
5.8
), the existence and uniqueness of
\beta^{(n+1)}
and the estimate (
5.39
) can be proven in exactly the same manner as in Lemma
4.10
and we omit the details.

We begin by the proof of (
5.40
). We take the divergence of (
5.2
) to get

(5.44)

\Delta(\beta^{(n+1)})^{i}=2\delta^{i\ell}\delta^{jk}\partial_{k}\left(e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}N^{(n)}(H^{(n)})_{j\ell}\right).

Note that the RHS has 0 mean (by Lemma
4.5
) and therefore by Theorem
B.1
, in order to prove (
5.40
), it suffices to bound the RHS of (
5.44
) in
H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}
by
CC_{i}
. Using (
4.1
), (
5.16
), (
5.8
) and
\varepsilon\left|\chi\ln\right|\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}
and taking
\varepsilon
small enough, we get :

(5.45)

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{k}\left(e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}N^{(n)}(H^{(n)})_{j\ell}\right)\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|N^{(n)}H^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2\varepsilon^{2}+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|H^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta+1}}\left(1+\left\|\tilde{N}^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta}}\right)+\left\|H^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2\varepsilon^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{i}.

We now turn to the proof of (
5.41
). We have
e_{0}^{(n)}\beta^{(n+1)}=\partial_{t}\beta^{(n+1)}-\beta^{(n)}\cdot\nabla\beta^{(n+1)}
. Using (
5.11
), (
5.39
) and the product estimate we have

\left\|\beta^{(n)}\cdot\nabla\beta^{(n+1)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}}}\lesssim\left\|\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}}}\left\|\beta^{(n+1)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}.

Applying
\partial_{t}
to (
5.2
),
\partial_{t}\beta^{(n+1)}
satisfy
(L\partial_{t}\beta^{(n+1)})_{ij}=2\partial_{t}(e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}N^{(n)}(H^{(n)})_{ij})
. We apply (
5.37
) with
\sigma=\delta^{\prime}+1
and use
|\chi\ln|\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{\eta}
(where
\eta
is as small as we want) :

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\partial_{t}\beta^{(n+1)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\partial_{t}\left(e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}N^{(n)}(H^{(n)})_{ij}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\gamma}^{(n)}H^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{1+2\varepsilon^{2}+\eta}}+\left\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\gamma}^{(n)}\tilde{N}^{(n)}H^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{1+2\varepsilon^{2}}}+\left\|\partial_{t}N_{a}^{(n)}H^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{1+2\varepsilon^{2}+\eta}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\|\partial_{t}\tilde{N}^{(n)}H^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{1+2\varepsilon^{2}}}+\left\|\partial_{t}H^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{1+2\varepsilon^{2}+\eta}}+\left\|\tilde{N}^{(n)}\partial_{t}H^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{1+2\varepsilon^{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon(1+C_{i}),

where used (
5.23
), (
5.8
), (
5.9
), (
5.29
) and (
5.17
) (with
\varepsilon
and
\eta
small enough, depending on
\lambda
).

We now turn to the proof of (
5.42
) and (
5.43
). Applying
e_{0}^{(n)}
to (
5.44
), we show that the following equation is satisfied :

(5.46)

\Delta(e_{0}^{(n)}\beta^{(n+1)})^{i}=2\delta^{i\ell}\delta^{jk}e_{0}^{(n)}\partial_{k}\left(e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}N^{(n)}(H^{(n)})_{j\ell}\right)+\left[\Delta,e_{0}^{(n)}\right](\beta^{(n+1)})^{i}=\vcentcolon I+II.

It’s easy to check the RHS of (
5.46
) has 0 mean, as a consequence we can apply Theorem
B.1
, so that in order to prove the estimate (
5.42
), it suffices to bound the RHS of (
5.46
) in
L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}
by
C_{i}
:

•

For
I
, we first commute
\nabla
and
e_{0}^{(n)}
:

(5.47)

|I|\lesssim\left|\nabla e_{0}^{(n)}\left(e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}N^{(n)}H^{(n)}\right)\right|+\left|\nabla\beta^{(n)}\right|\left|\nabla\left(e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}N^{(n)}H^{(n)}\right)\right|.

It implies, using (
4.1
) :

(5.48)

\displaystyle\|I\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|(e_{0}^{(n)}\gamma^{(n)})N^{(n)}H^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+2\varepsilon^{2}+1}}+\left\|(e_{0}^{(n)}N^{(n)})H^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+2\varepsilon^{2}+1}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\|N^{(n)}(e_{0}^{(n)}H^{(n)})\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+2\varepsilon^{2}+1}}+\left\|\nabla\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|N^{(n)}H^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+2\varepsilon^{2}+1}}.

Thanks to (
5.12
) and (
5.29
), the last term is bounded by
\varepsilon A_{0}C_{i}
. Thanks to (
5.18
), the third term is handled as
N^{(n)}H^{(n)}
in (
5.45
) and is therefore bounded by
A_{0}C_{i}
. Thanks to (
5.8
), (
5.9
), (
5.11
), (
5.16
) and (
5.29
), the second term is bounded by
C_{i}
. The first term is similar to the second, and actually easier to bound, so we omit the details. We have shown that
\|I\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}\lesssim A_{0}C_{i}
.

•

For
II
, we use the following commutation estimate :

(5.49)

\left|\left[\Delta,e_{0}^{(n)}\right](\beta^{(n+1)})^{i}\right|\lesssim\left|\nabla\beta^{(n)}\right|\left|\nabla^{2}\beta^{(n+1)}\right|+\left|\nabla^{2}\beta^{(n)}\right|\left|\nabla\beta^{(n+1)}\right|.

Now, using in addition (
5.11
), (
5.12
), (
5.39
) and (
5.40
) and the product estimate :

\displaystyle\left\|II\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\left\|\nabla^{2}\beta^{(n+1)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}+\left\|\nabla^{2}\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}\left\|\nabla\beta^{(n+1)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon A_{0}C_{i}.

Similarly, in order to prove (
5.43
), we have to bound the RHS of (
5.46
) in
H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}
by
CC_{i}^{2}
:

•

For
I
, we again use (
5.47
). Instead of using
L^{\infty}
bounds for
\nabla\beta^{(n)}
, we use the product estimate and then (
4.1
). For the terms where
e_{0}^{(n)}
appears, we simply use (
4.1
) :

\displaystyle\|I\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|(e_{0}^{(n)}\gamma^{(n)})N^{(n)}H^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2\varepsilon^{2}+1}}+\left\|(e_{0}^{(n)}N^{(n)})H^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2\varepsilon^{2}+1}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\|N^{(n)}(e_{0}^{(n)}H^{(n)})\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2\varepsilon^{2}+1}}+\left\|\nabla\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\left\|N^{(n)}H^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2\varepsilon^{2}+1}}.

The last term is handled thanks to (
5.12
) and (
5.45
) and is indeed bounded by
A_{0}C_{i}^{2}
. The third term is similar to the last one (because
e_{0}^{(n)}H^{(n)}
satisfies (
5.19
)) and is therefore handled as in (
5.45
), finally it is bounded by
A_{3}C_{i}^{2}
. We handled the two first terms as we did in (
5.48
), using (
5.16
) instead of (
5.29
), this change explains why we get
C_{i}^{2}
instead of
C_{i}
.

•

For
II
, we use again the commutation estimate (
5.49
), (
5.12
) and (
5.40
) and the product estimate :

\displaystyle\left\|\left[\Delta,e_{0}^{(n)}\right](\beta^{(n+1)})^{i}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\left\|\nabla^{2}\beta^{(n+1)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}+\left\|\nabla^{2}\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}\left\|\nabla\beta^{(n+1)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim A_{0}^{2}C_{i}^{2}.

∎

We have finished all the elliptic estimates, in the sequel we deal with evolution equations, and we will use the freedom of taking
T
as small as we want, in order to recover our estimates.

5.2.3.
The transport equation and
\tau^{(n+1)}

We begin this section by prooving the estimates on
H^{(n+1)}
. We first prove a technical lemma about the transport equation :

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
. If
f
and
h
satisfy

e_{0}^{(n+1)}f=h,

then,

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}(t)\leq 2\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}(0)+2\sqrt{T}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}(t).

Proof.

Let
w(x)=\langle x\rangle^{2\sigma}
. We multiply the equation
e_{0}^{(n+1)}f=h
by
wf
and integrate over
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Writing
e_{0}^{(n+1)}=\partial_{t}-\beta^{(n+1)}\cdot\nabla
, we get :

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}^{2}\right)=2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}wfh\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}w\beta^{(n+1)}\cdot\nabla\left(f^{2}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x.

We integrate by part the last term in order to get :

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}wfh\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}f^{2}\mathrm{div}\left(w\beta^{(n+1)}\right)\mathrm{d}x.

For the last term, we use (
5.40
) (and the embedding
H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}\xhookrightarrow{}C^{0}_{1}
) and
\left|\nabla w\right|\lesssim\frac{w}{\langle x\rangle}
to obtain :

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}f^{2}\mathrm{div}\left(w\beta^{(n+1)}\right)\mathrm{d}x\lesssim C_{i}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}

For the first term, we simply use the Cauchy-Scwharz inequality and
2ab\leq a^{2}+b^{2}
to obtain :

2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}wfh\,\mathrm{d}x\leq 2\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}wf^{2}\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}wh^{2}\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}^{2}+\|h\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}^{2}.

Summarising, we get :

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}^{2}\right)\leq C(C_{i})\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}^{2}+\|h\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}^{2}.

We apply Gronwall’s Lemma, take
T
small enough and use
\sqrt{a^{2}+b^{2}}\leq a+b
to get :

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}(t)\leq 2\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}(0)+2\sqrt{T}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}(t).

∎

Proposition 5.5
.

For
n\geq 2
, the following estimates hold :

(5.50)

\displaystyle\left\|H^{(n+1)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta+1}}

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{i},

(5.51)

\displaystyle\left\|e_{0}^{(n+1)}H^{(n+1)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{1+\lambda}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{2},

(5.52)

\displaystyle\left\|e_{0}^{(n+1)}H^{(n+1)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{i},

(5.53)

\displaystyle\left\|e_{0}^{(n+1)}H^{(n+1)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim A_{2}C_{i}^{2}.

Proof.

To prove (
5.51
) we just bound the RHS of (
5.4
) in
L^{2}_{\delta+1}
using the weighted product estimates
H^{1}\times H^{1}\xhookrightarrow{}L^{2}
and using weighted
L^{\infty}
estimates for
N^{(n)}
in the first and last terms. More concretely, we use (
5.8
), (
5.29
), (
5.11
), (
5.30
), (
5.28
), (
4.1
) and (
4.3
), and we recall that
\lambda<\delta+1
:

\displaystyle\left\|e_{0}^{(n+1)}H^{(n+1)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{1+\lambda}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|N^{(n)}(H^{(n)})^{2}\right\|_{L^{2}_{1+\lambda}}+\left\|\nabla\beta^{(n)}H^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{1+\lambda}}+\left\|\nabla^{2}N^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{1+\lambda}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\|\nabla\gamma^{(n)}\nabla N^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{1+\lambda}}+\left\|N^{(n)}(\nabla\varphi^{(n)})^{2}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|N^{(n)}(\nabla\omega^{(n)})^{2}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}.

We continue by the proof of (
5.52
) and (
5.53
), which amounts to bounding the
H^{1}_{\delta+1}
and
H^{2}_{\delta+1}
norms of the RHS of (
5.4
). First notice that the terms
e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}N^{(n)}(H^{(n)})_{i}^{\;\,\ell}(H^{(n)})_{j\ell}
,
(\partial_{i}\varphi^{(n)}\bar{\otimes}\partial_{j}\varphi^{(n)})N^{(n)}
and
(\partial_{i}\omega^{(n)}\bar{\otimes}\partial_{j}\omega^{(n)})N^{(n)}
are analogous to terms in (
5.1
) (because
\nabla\varphi^{(n)}
and
\partial_{t}\varphi^{(n)}
satisfy the same estimates, samewise for
\omega^{(n)}
), and can be treated as in Proposition
5.3
. We recall the estimates obtained :

\displaystyle\left\|e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}N^{(n)}(H^{(n)})_{i}^{\;\,\ell}(H^{(n)})_{j\ell}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}C_{i},

\displaystyle\left\|e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}N^{(n)}(H^{(n)})_{i}^{\;\,\ell}(H^{(n)})_{j\ell}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}C(A_{2})C_{i}^{2},

\displaystyle\left\|(\partial_{i}\varphi^{(n)}\bar{\otimes}\partial_{j}\varphi^{(n)})N^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta+1}}+\left\|e^{-4\varphi}(\partial_{i}\omega^{(n)}\bar{\otimes}\partial_{j}\omega^{(n)})N^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon C(A_{0})C_{i},

\displaystyle\left\|(\partial_{i}\varphi^{(n)}\bar{\otimes}\partial_{j}\varphi^{(n)})N^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta+1}}+\left\|e^{-4\varphi}(\partial_{i}\omega^{(n)}\bar{\otimes}\partial_{j}\omega^{(n)})N^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}C(A_{2})C_{i}^{2}+C(A_{0})C_{i}^{2}.

The remaining terms are treated as follows :

•

We first use (
5.16
) and (
5.11
) and the product estimate :

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{(j}(\beta^{(n)})^{k}(H^{(n)})_{i)k}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|H^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta+1}}\left\|\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\lesssim\varepsilon C_{i}.

Then we use (
5.29
), (
5.16
) and (
5.12
) and the product estimate :

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{(j}(\beta^{(n)})^{k}(H^{(n)})_{i)k}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla\beta^{(n)}H^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta+1}}+\left\|\nabla^{3}\beta^{(n)}H^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta+3}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\|\nabla^{2}\beta^{(n)}\nabla H^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta+3}}+\left\|\nabla\beta^{(n)}\nabla^{2}H^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta+3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\left\|H^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta+1}}+\left\|\nabla^{3}\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+3}}\left\|H^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta+1}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\|\nabla^{2}\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}\left\|\nabla H^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta+2}}+\left\|\nabla\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\left\|\nabla^{2}H^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta+3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}A_{0}C_{i}+A_{0}C_{i}^{2}.

•

We use (
5.9
) and the fact that
\langle x\rangle^{\alpha}\in L^{2}
if and only if
\alpha<-1
:

\left\|(\partial_{i}\bar{\otimes}\partial_{j})N^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta+1}}\leq\left\|\nabla^{2}\tilde{N}^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta+1}}+\left|N_{a}^{(n)}\right|\left\|\nabla^{2}(\chi\ln)\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta+1}}\lesssim C_{i}.

We then use (
5.10
) for the
H^{2}
estimate :

\left\|(\partial_{i}\bar{\otimes}\partial_{j})N^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta+1}}\leq\left\|\nabla^{2}\tilde{N}^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta+1}}+\left|N_{a}^{(n)}\right|\left\|\nabla^{2}(\chi\ln)\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta+1}}\lesssim A_{2}C_{i}^{2}.

•

For the following term, we get both
H^{1}
and
H^{2}
estimates by using (
5.8
), (
5.9
) and (
5.23
) and the product estimate :

\displaystyle\left\|(\delta_{i}^{k}\bar{\otimes}\partial_{j}\gamma^{(n)})\partial_{k}N^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta+1}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\nabla\tilde{\gamma}^{(n)}\nabla\tilde{N}^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta+1}}+|\alpha|\left\|\nabla(\chi\ln)\nabla\tilde{N}^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta+1}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left|N_{a}^{(n)}\right|\left\|\nabla(\chi\ln)\nabla\tilde{\gamma}^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta+1}}+|\alpha|\left|N_{a}^{(n)}\right|\left\|(\nabla(\chi\ln))^{2}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla\tilde{\gamma}^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta+1}}\left\|\nabla\tilde{N}^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta+1}}+\varepsilon\left(\left\|\nabla\tilde{N}^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta}}+\left\|\nabla\tilde{\gamma}^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\varepsilon^{2}\left\|\langle x\rangle^{\delta-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon C_{i}^{2}.

We now prove (
5.50
). We recall the following commutation formula :

\displaystyle\left|\left[e_{0}^{(n+1)},\nabla\right]H^{(n+1)}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\nabla\beta^{(n+1)}\right|\left|\nabla H^{(n+1)}\right|,

\displaystyle\left|\left[e_{0}^{(n+1)},\nabla^{2}\right]H^{(n+1)}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\nabla\beta^{(n+1)}\right|\left|\nabla^{2}H^{(n+1)}\right|+\left|\nabla^{2}\beta^{(n+1)}\right|\left|\nabla H^{(n+1)}\right|.

Hence, using (
5.40
) :

(5.54)

\left\|e_{0}^{(n+1)}\nabla^{\alpha}H^{(n+1)}_{ij}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta+1+|\alpha|}}\lesssim\left\|e_{0}^{(n+1)}H^{(n+1)}_{ij}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta+1}}+C_{i}\left\|H^{(n+1)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta+1}}

where
|\alpha|\leq 2
. We apply the Lemma
5.2
with
\sigma=\delta+1+|\alpha|
and
f=\nabla^{\alpha}H^{(n+1)}
:

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|\nabla^{\alpha}H^{(n+1)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta+1+|\alpha|}}(t)

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|\nabla^{\alpha}H^{(n+1)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta+1+|\alpha|}}(0)+2\sqrt{T}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|e_{0}^{(n+1)}\nabla^{\alpha}H^{(n+1)}_{ij}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta+1+|\alpha|}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2\left\|\nabla^{\alpha}H^{(n+1)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta+1+|\alpha|}}(0)+2\sqrt{T}C_{i}^{2}+2C_{i}\sqrt{T}\left\|H^{(n+1)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta+1}},

where in the last inequality we use (
5.54
) and (
5.53
).

We sum over all
|\alpha|\leq 2
and absorb the term
\left\|H^{(n+1)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta+1}}
of the RHS into the LHS (choosing
T
small enough). Recalling that
\left\|H^{(n+1)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta+1}}(0)\leq C_{i}
ends the proof of (
5.50
).

∎

Next, we prove the estimates for
\tau^{(n+1)}
, gathered in the following proposition :

Proposition 5.6
.

For
n\geq 2
, the following estimates hold :

(5.55)

\displaystyle\left\|\tau^{(n+1)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim A_{0}C_{i},

(5.56)

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{t}\tau^{(n+1)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim A_{1}C_{i},

(5.57)

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{t}\tau^{(n+1)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim A_{2}C_{i}.

Proof.

In view of
(
5.3
)
, the estimates for
\tau^{(n+1)}
can be obtained by directly controlling

-2\mathbf{T}^{(n-1)}\gamma^{(n)}+\frac{\mathrm{div}\left(\beta^{(n)}\right)}{N^{(n-1)}}.

We bound the two terms separately, using first (
5.12
), (
5.8
), (
5.9
) and
\left|\frac{1}{N^{(n-1)}}\right|\lesssim 1
:

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\mathrm{div}\left(\beta^{(n)}\right)}{N^{(n-1)}}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{3}_{\delta^{\prime}}}+\left\|\nabla\tilde{N}^{(n-1)}\nabla\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\|\nabla^{2}\tilde{N}^{(n-1)}\nabla\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+3}}+\left\|\nabla\tilde{N}^{(n-1)}\nabla^{2}\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{3}_{\delta^{\prime}}}+\left\|\nabla\tilde{N}^{(n-1)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta+1}}\left\|\nabla\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\|\nabla^{2}\tilde{N}^{(n-1)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta+2}}\left\|\nabla\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}+\left\|\nabla\tilde{N}^{(n-1)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta+1}}\left\|\nabla^{2}\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+\varepsilon)A_{0}C_{i}.

Using in addition Proposition
5.1
we get :

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{T}^{(n-1)}\gamma^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\leq\left\|\mathbf{T}^{(n-1)}\tilde{\gamma}^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}+|\alpha|\left\|\frac{\beta^{(n-1)}\cdot\nabla(\chi\ln)}{N^{(n-1)}}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\lesssim(1+\varepsilon)C_{i},

which concludes the proof of (
5.55
).

We now turn to the estimates concerning
\partial_{t}\tau^{(n+1)}
, which has the following expression :

\partial_{t}\tau^{(n+1)}=-2\partial_{t}\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n-1)}\tilde{\gamma}^{(n)}\right)+2\alpha\nabla(\chi\ln)\cdot\partial_{t}\left(\frac{\beta^{(n-1)}}{N^{(n-1)}}\right)+\partial_{t}\left(\frac{\mathrm{div}(\beta^{(n)})}{N^{(n-1)}}\right)

By (
5.24
),
\left\|\partial_{t}(\mathbf{T}^{(n-1)}\tilde{\gamma}^{(n)})\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\leq A_{0}C_{i}
. Then, we note, that thanks to (
5.11
) and (
5.14
), we have
\|\partial_{t}\beta^{(n)}\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\lesssim A_{1}C_{i}
(and the same with
n
replaced by
n-1
). For the second term, we do the following :

\displaystyle\left\|2\alpha\nabla(\chi\ln)\cdot\partial_{t}\left(\frac{\beta^{(n-1)}}{N^{(n-1)}}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\;\varepsilon\left(\left\|\partial_{t}\beta^{(n-1)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}+\left|\partial_{t}N_{a}^{(n-1)}\right|\left\|\beta^{(n-1)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}}}+\left\|\partial_{t}\tilde{N}^{(n-1)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta}}\left\|\beta^{(n-1)}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)

(5.58)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon A_{1}C_{i},

where we used (
5.9
) and (
5.11
). The third term is very similar :

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{t}\left(\frac{\mathrm{div}(\beta^{(n)})}{N^{(n-1)}}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\partial_{t}\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}}}+\left\|\nabla\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\left(\left\|\partial_{t}\tilde{N}^{(n-1)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta}}+\left|\partial_{t}N_{a}^{(n-1)}\right|\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim A_{1}C_{i}.

This finishes the proof of (
5.56
).

We now turn to the proof of (
5.57
). In view of (
5.56
), we just have to bound
\|\nabla\partial_{t}\tau^{(n+1)}\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}
by
C_{i}^{2}
. We have the following expression :

\displaystyle\nabla\partial_{t}\tau^{(n+1)}

\displaystyle=-2\nabla\partial_{t}\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n-1)}\tilde{\gamma}^{(n)}\right)+2\alpha\nabla^{2}(\chi\ln)\cdot\partial_{t}\left(\frac{\beta^{(n-1)}}{N^{(n-1)}}\right)+2\alpha\nabla(\chi\ln)\partial_{t}\left(\frac{\nabla\beta^{(n-1)}}{N^{(n-1)}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-2\alpha\nabla(\chi\ln)\partial_{t}\left(\frac{\beta^{(n-1)}\nabla N^{(n-1)}}{\left(N^{(n-1)}\right)^{2}}\right)+\partial_{t}\left(\frac{\mathrm{div}(\nabla\beta^{(n)})}{N^{(n-1)}}\right)-\partial_{t}\left(\frac{\nabla N^{(n-1)}\mathrm{div}(\beta^{(n)})}{\left(N^{(n-1)}\right)^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\vcentcolon I+II+III+IV+V+VI.

The term
I
is easily handle thanks to (
5.25
) : we have
\left\|I\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}\leq A_{2}C_{i}
. For the other terms, we make the following remarks :

•

the term
VI
is worse than the term
IV
,

•

the term
V
is worse than the terms
II
and
III
.

Thus, it only remains to bound the terms
V
and
VI
, for which we use (
5.9
), (
5.11
) and (
5.14
) :

\displaystyle\left\|V\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\partial_{t}\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}}}+\left\|\nabla^{2}\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}\left\|\partial_{t}\tilde{N}^{(n-1)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta}}\lesssim A_{1}C_{i}+\varepsilon C_{i}\lesssim A_{1}C_{i}.

\displaystyle\left\|VI\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla\tilde{N}^{(n-1)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta+1}}\left\|\partial_{t}\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}}}+\left\|\nabla\partial_{t}\tilde{N}^{(n-1)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta+1}}\left\|\nabla\beta^{(n-1)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\|\partial_{t}\tilde{N}^{(n-1)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta}}\left\|\nabla\tilde{N}^{(n-1)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta+1}}\left\|\nabla\beta^{(n-1)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon C_{i}.

This concludes the proof of (
5.57
).

∎

5.2.4.
Energy estimate for
\Box_{g^{(n)}}

In this section, we establish the usual energy estimate for the operator
\Box_{g^{(n)}}
.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
. If
h
is a solution of

(5.59)

\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\right)^{2}h-e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}\Delta h=f,

then, if
T
is sufficiently small, we have for all
t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{T}^{(n)}h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}(t)

\displaystyle+\left\|e^{-\gamma^{(n)}}\nabla h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}(t)

(5.60)

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\left\|\mathbf{T}^{(n)}h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}(0)+\left\|e^{-\gamma^{(n)}}\nabla h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}(0)+\sqrt{2T}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\left\|fN^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}\right).

Proof.

Let
w(x)=\langle x\rangle^{2\sigma}
. We multiply the equation by
we_{0}^{(n)}h
and we integrate over
\mathbb{R}^{2}
with respect to
\mathrm{d}x
. After integration by parts we obtain :

(5.61)

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{w}{2}e_{0}^{(n)}\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}h\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla h\cdot\nabla\left(e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}we_{0}^{(n)}h\right)\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}wfe_{0}^{(n)}h\,\mathrm{d}x.

We define the energy
E(t)\vcentcolon=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}w\left(\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}h\right)^{2}+e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}|\nabla h|^{2}\right)(t,x)\mathrm{d}x
and compute its time derivative, writing
\partial_{t}=e_{0}^{(n)}+\beta^{(n)}\cdot\nabla
and integrating by parts the terms coming from
\beta^{(n)}\cdot\nabla
:

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}E}{\mathrm{d}t}(t)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}we_{0}^{(n)}\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}h\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}we_{0}^{(n)}

\displaystyle\left(e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}|\nabla h|^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathrm{div}(w\beta^{(n)})\left(\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}h\right)^{2}+e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}|\nabla h|^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}x

We now use (
5.61
) to express the first integral in
\frac{\mathrm{d}E}{\mathrm{d}t}
:

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}E}{\mathrm{d}t}(t)

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}wfe_{0}^{(n)}h\,\mathrm{d}x-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla h\cdot\nabla\left(e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}we_{0}^{(n)}h\right)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}we_{0}^{(n)}\left(e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}|\nabla h|^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathrm{div}(w\beta^{(n)})\left(\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}h\right)^{2}+e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}|\nabla h|^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}x

We now expand the second integral and commute
\nabla
and
e_{0}^{(n)}
:

\displaystyle-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla h\cdot\nabla\left(e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}we_{0}^{(n)}h\right)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}we^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}e_{0}^{(n)}\left(|\nabla h|^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}x+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}we^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}\partial_{i}h\nabla h\cdot\nabla\beta^{(n)i}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\quad-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}e_{0}^{(n)}h\nabla h\cdot\nabla w\mathrm{d}x-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}we_{0}^{(n)}h\nabla h\cdot\nabla\left(e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}\right)\mathrm{d}x

With this, we see that the
\partial h\partial^{2}h
terms in
\frac{\mathrm{d}E}{\mathrm{d}t}
cancel each other. Thus, we obtain the following energy equality :

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}E}{\mathrm{d}t}(t)

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathrm{div}(w\beta^{(n)})\left(\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}h\right)^{2}+e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}|\nabla h|^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}x+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}we^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}\partial_{i}h\nabla h\cdot\nabla\beta^{(n)i}\mathrm{d}x

(5.62)

\displaystyle\quad-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}e_{0}^{(n)}h\nabla h\cdot\nabla w\mathrm{d}x+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}wfe_{0}^{(n)}h\,\mathrm{d}x+R_{\gamma^{(n)}}(t)

with

R_{\gamma^{(n)}}(t)\vcentcolon=-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}w|\nabla h|^{2}\partial_{t}\gamma^{(n)}\mathrm{d}x+4\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}we_{0}^{(n)}h\nabla h\cdot\nabla\gamma^{(n)}\mathrm{d}x-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}w|\nabla h|^{2}\beta^{(n)}\cdot\nabla\gamma^{(n)}\mathrm{d}x.

This sort of remainder contains all the term involving derivatives of
\gamma^{(n)}
, therefore it would vanish if
e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}
didn’t appear in (
5.59
). We then show that the first three integrals in (
5.62
) can be bounded by
E(t)
:

•

Let’s show that
|\mathrm{div}(w\beta^{(n)})|\leq C(C_{i})w
. We have
\mathrm{div}(w\beta^{(n)})=w\mathrm{div}(\beta^{(n)})+\nabla w\cdot\beta^{(n)}
. We have
|\nabla w|\lesssim\frac{w}{\langle x\rangle}
and
\beta^{(n)}
bounded so
\nabla w\cdot\beta^{(n)}
is indeed bounded by
w
. For
w\mathrm{div}(\beta^{(n)})
, we use the embedding
H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}\xhookrightarrow{}L^{\infty}
and the estimate (
5.12
). This shows that

-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathrm{div}(w\beta^{(n)})\left(\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}h\right)^{2}+e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}\nabla h\right)\mathrm{d}x\lesssim A_{0}C_{i}E(t).

•

Let’s show that
|e^{-\gamma^{(n)}}N^{(n)}\nabla w|\lesssim w
. We have
|e^{-\gamma^{(n)}}|\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{\varepsilon^{2}}
and
|N^{(n)}|\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}
so
|e^{-\gamma^{(n)}}N^{(n)}\nabla w|\lesssim w\langle x\rangle^{\varepsilon^{2}-\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim w
, providing
\varepsilon
is small. This allows us to do the following (using
2ab\leq a^{2}+b^{2}
) :

-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}e_{0}^{(n)}h\nabla h\cdot\nabla w\,\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}w\left|\frac{e_{0}^{(n)}h}{N^{(n)}}\right|e^{-\gamma^{(n)}}|\nabla h|\,\mathrm{d}x\lesssim E(t).

•

We already used the fact that
\nabla\beta^{(n)}
is bounded by
A_{0}C_{i}
so we simply do

2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}w\partial_{i}h\nabla h\cdot\nabla\beta^{(n)i}\,\mathrm{d}x\lesssim A_{0}C_{i}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}w|\nabla h|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\lesssim A_{0}C_{i}E(t).

We now show that
R_{\gamma^{(n)}}(t)
can also be bounded by
E(t)
:

•

Let’s show that
\partial_{t}\gamma^{(n)}
is bounded. Since
\alpha
doesn’t depend on time, we have
\partial_{t}\gamma^{(n)}=N^{(n-1)}\mathbf{T}^{(n-1)}\tilde{\gamma}^{(n)}+\beta^{(n)}\cdot\nabla\tilde{\gamma}^{(n)}
. For the first term we use the Proposition
5.1
and the embedding
H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}\xhookrightarrow{}C^{0}_{1}
(together with the fact that
|N^{(n-1)}|\lesssim\langle x\rangle
). For the second term we simply use the embedding
H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}}\xhookrightarrow{}L^{\infty}
. We thus get

2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}w|\nabla h|^{2}\partial_{t}\gamma^{(n)}\mathrm{d}x\leq C(C_{i})E(t).

•

Let’s show that
e^{-\gamma^{(n)}}N^{(n)}\nabla\gamma^{(n)}
is bounded. We only deal with the
\chi\ln
part of
N^{(n)}
(since
\tilde{N}^{(n)}
is bounded), and only with the
\nabla\tilde{\gamma}^{(n)}
part in
\nabla\gamma^{(n)}
(because
\nabla(\chi\ln)
decrease more than
e^{-\gamma^{(n)}}
). Using
|\chi\ln|\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{\varepsilon^{2}}
and
e^{-\gamma^{(n)}}\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{\varepsilon^{2}}
, we write

\left|e^{-\gamma^{(n)}}\chi\ln\nabla\tilde{\gamma}^{(n)}\right|\lesssim\|\nabla\tilde{\gamma}^{(n)}\|_{C^{0}_{2\varepsilon^{2}}}.

If
\varepsilon
is small enough we have the embedding
H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}\xhookrightarrow{}C^{0}_{2\varepsilon^{2}}
which together with (
5.23
) allows us to say

4\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}we_{0}^{(n)}h\nabla h\cdot\nabla\gamma^{(n)}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim C(C_{i})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}w\left|\frac{e_{0}^{(n)}h}{N^{(n)}}\right|e^{-\gamma^{(n)}}|\nabla h|\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C(C_{i})E(t).

•

We already used multiple times that
\beta^{(n)}
and
\nabla\gamma^{(n)}
are bounded (by
\varepsilon
and
C(C_{i})
respectively), and thus

2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}w|\nabla h|^{2}\beta^{(n)}\cdot\nabla\gamma^{(n)}\mathrm{d}x\leq C(C_{i})E(t).

For the last integral in (
5.62
) we apply Cauchy-Schwarz inequality :

2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}wfe_{0}^{(n)}h\,\mathrm{d}x\leq 2\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}wf^{2}N^{(n)2}\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}w\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}h\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq E(t)+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}wf^{2}N^{(n)2}\mathrm{d}x.

Summarising all the estimates, we get :

\frac{\mathrm{d}E}{\mathrm{d}t}(t)\leq C(C_{i})E(t)+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}wf^{2}N^{(n)2}(t,x)\,\mathrm{d}x.

We apply Gronwall’s inequality with
T
sufficiently small to obtain

E(t)\leq 2\left(E(0)+T\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}wf^{2}N^{(n)2}(s,x)\,\mathrm{d}x\right).

We recognize in
E(t)
a weighted Sobolev norm, and using inequality such as
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(a+b)\leq\sqrt{a^{2}+b^{2}}\leq a+b
, we obtain the inequality of the lemma.

∎

Lemma 5.4
.

If
h
is a solution of (
5.59
) then, if
T
is sufficiently small, we have for all
t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 2}\left(\left\|\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\nabla^{\alpha}h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1+|\alpha|}}(t)+\left\|e^{-\gamma^{(n)}}\nabla(\nabla^{\alpha}h)\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1+|\alpha|}}(t)\right)

(5.63)

\displaystyle\leq 3\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 2}\left(\left\|\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\nabla^{\alpha}h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1+|\alpha|}}(0)+\left\|e^{-\gamma^{(n)}}\nabla(\nabla^{\alpha}h)\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1+|\alpha|}}(0)\right)+C(C_{i})\sqrt{T}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\left\|fN^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}(s).

Proof.

For the sake of clarity, we set

\mathcal{E}^{(n)}[h](t)=\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 2}\left(\left\|\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\nabla^{\alpha}h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1+|\alpha|}}(t)+\left\|e^{-\gamma^{(n)}}\nabla(\nabla^{\alpha}h)\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1+|\alpha|}}(t)\right)

If
h
satisfies (
5.59
), then, applying
\nabla^{\alpha}
to the equation, we show that
\nabla^{\alpha}h
satisfies

(5.64)

\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\right)^{2}\nabla^{\alpha}h-e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}\Delta(\nabla^{\alpha}h)=\nabla^{\alpha}f+\left[\nabla^{\alpha},e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}\right]\Delta h+\left[\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\right)^{2},\nabla^{\alpha}\right]h.

Thanks to the previous lemma, in order to prove (
5.63
), we have to bound
\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 2}\|(\text{RHS of \eqref{eq derivee}})\times N^{(n)}\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1+|\alpha|}}
.

•

First step is bounding
\|N^{(n)}\nabla^{\alpha}f\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1+|\alpha|}}
(using the fact that
\frac{1}{N^{(n)}}\in L^{\infty}
,
|\nabla^{\alpha}(\chi\ln)|\leq\langle x\rangle^{-|\alpha|}
, the product estimates and (
5.9
)). If
|\alpha|=1
:

\displaystyle\|N^{(n)}\nabla^{\alpha}f\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla^{\alpha}(fN^{(n)})\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}+\|f\nabla^{\alpha}N^{(n)}\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|fN^{(n)}\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}+\|N^{(n)}f\nabla^{\alpha}(\chi\ln)\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}+\|N^{(n)}f\nabla^{\alpha}\tilde{N}^{(n)}\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|fN^{(n)}\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\left(2+\|\nabla^{\alpha}\tilde{N}^{(n)}\|_{H^{2}_{\delta+1}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(C_{i})\|fN^{(n)}\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}.

If
|\alpha|=2
, then there exist
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}
with
|\alpha_{1}|=|\alpha_{2}|=1
such that :

\|N^{(n)}\nabla^{\alpha}f\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+3}}\lesssim\|\nabla^{\alpha}(fN^{(n)})\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+3}}+\|f\nabla^{\alpha}N^{(n)}\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+3}}+\|\nabla^{\alpha_{1}}N^{(n)}\nabla^{\alpha_{2}}f\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+3}}.

The two first terms can be handled as in the case
|\alpha|=1
. For the last term we do the following :

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{\alpha_{1}}N^{(n)}\nabla^{\alpha_{2}}f\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|N^{(n)}\nabla^{\alpha_{1}}N^{(n)}\nabla^{\alpha_{2}}f\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|N^{(n)}\nabla^{\alpha_{2}}f\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}\|\nabla^{\alpha_{1}}N^{(n)}\|_{H^{2}_{\delta+1}}

\displaystyle\leq C(C_{i})\|fN^{(n)}\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}},

where in the last inequality we use the calculation of the
|\alpha|=1
case. Summarising, we get :

(5.65)

\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 2}\|N^{(n)}\nabla^{\alpha}f\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1+|\alpha|}}\leq C(C_{i})\|fN^{(n)}\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}.

•

Second step is bounding
\left\|N^{(n)}\left[\nabla^{\alpha},e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}\right]\Delta h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1+|\alpha|}}
. If
|\alpha|=1
we have
\left[\nabla^{\alpha},e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}\right]\Delta h=-2e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}\nabla^{\alpha}\gamma^{(n)}\Delta h
.
Using the fact that
\left|\tilde{N}^{(n)}\right|\leq\varepsilon
, (
4.1
),
|\chi\ln|\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{\varepsilon^{2}}
and the expression of
\gamma^{(n)}
we have

\displaystyle\left\|e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}N^{(n)}\nabla^{\alpha}\gamma^{(n)}\Delta h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla^{\alpha}\gamma^{(n)}\Delta h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2+3\varepsilon^{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla^{\alpha}(\chi\ln)\Delta h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2+3\varepsilon^{2}}}+\left\|\nabla^{\alpha}\tilde{\gamma}^{(n)}\Delta h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2+3\varepsilon^{2}}}.

Since
|\alpha|=1
, we have
|\nabla^{\alpha}(\chi\ln)|\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{-1}
and
\nabla^{\alpha}\tilde{\gamma}^{(n)}\in H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}
which embeds in
C^{0}_{1}
. This implies :

\left\|e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}N^{(n)}\nabla^{\alpha}\gamma^{(n)}\Delta h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}\leq C(C_{i})\|\Delta h\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1+3\varepsilon^{2}}}\leq C(C_{i})\sum_{|\alpha^{\prime}|=1}\left\|e^{-\gamma^{(n)}}\nabla(\nabla^{\alpha^{\prime}}h)\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}},

where in the last inequality, we used that
1\lesssim|e^{-\gamma^{(n)}}|
and took
\varepsilon
small enough. If
|\alpha|=2
, then there exist
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}
with
|\alpha_{1}|=|\alpha_{2}|=1
such that :

\displaystyle\left\|N^{(n)}\left[\nabla^{\alpha},e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}\right]\Delta h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}N^{(n)}\nabla^{\alpha}\gamma^{(n)}\Delta h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+3}}+\left\|e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}N^{(n)}\nabla^{\alpha_{1}}\gamma^{(n)}\nabla^{\alpha_{2}}\Delta h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+3}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}N^{(n)}\nabla^{\alpha_{1}}\gamma^{(n)}\nabla^{\alpha_{2}}\gamma^{(n)}\Delta h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla^{\alpha}\gamma^{(n)}\Delta h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+3+3\varepsilon^{2}}}+\left\|\nabla^{\alpha_{1}}\gamma^{(n)}\nabla^{\alpha_{2}}\Delta h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+3+3\varepsilon^{2}}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|\nabla^{\alpha_{1}}\gamma^{(n)}\nabla^{\alpha_{2}}\gamma^{(n)}\Delta h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+3+3\varepsilon^{2}}}

For the first term, we use the fact that
|\nabla^{\alpha}(\chi\ln)|\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{-2}
,
\nabla^{\alpha}\tilde{\gamma}^{(n)}\in H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}
(since
|\alpha|=2
) and the product estimate to get :

\left\|\nabla^{\alpha}\gamma^{(n)}\Delta h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+3+3\varepsilon^{2}}}\leq C(C_{i})\|\Delta h\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}\leq C(C_{i})\sum_{|\alpha^{\prime}|=1,2}\left\|e^{-\gamma^{(n)}}\nabla(\nabla^{\alpha^{\prime}}h)\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1+|\alpha^{\prime}|}}.

For the second term, we again use that
\nabla^{\alpha_{1}}\tilde{\gamma}^{(n)},\nabla^{\alpha_{1}}(\chi\ln)\in C^{0}_{1}
(since
|\alpha_{1}|=1
) to get

\left\|\nabla^{\alpha_{1}}\gamma^{(n)}\nabla^{\alpha_{2}}\Delta h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+3+3\varepsilon^{2}}}\leq C(C_{i})\|\nabla^{\alpha_{2}}\Delta h\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2+3\varepsilon^{2}}}\leq C(C_{i})\sum_{|\alpha^{\prime}|=2}\left\|e^{-\gamma^{(n)}}\nabla(\nabla^{\alpha^{\prime}}h)\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+3}}.

The third term is easier to handle than the first one. Summarising, we get :

(5.66)

\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 2}\left\|N^{(n)}\left[\nabla^{\alpha},e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}\right]\Delta h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1+|\alpha|}}\leq C(C_{i})\mathcal{E}^{(n)}[h].

•

Third step is bounding
\left\|N^{(n)}\left[\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\right)^{2},\nabla^{\alpha}\right]h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1+|\alpha|}}
.
Given the expression of
\mathcal{E}^{(n)}[h]
, we are allowed to bound this term by norms involving
\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\nabla^{\mu}
,
\nabla^{\nu}
(for
|\mu|\leq 2
and
|\nu|\leq 3
) and
\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\right)^{2}
. The strategy is then to express
N^{(n)}\left[\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\right)^{2},\nabla\right]h
and
N^{(n)}\left[\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\right)^{2},\nabla^{2}\right]h
in terms of those operators acting on
h
, using the commutation formula

\left[\mathbf{T}^{(n)},\nabla\right]h=\frac{\nabla\beta^{(n)}}{N^{(n)}}\nabla h-\frac{\nabla N^{(n)}}{N^{(n)}}\mathbf{T}^{(n)}h.

Doing so, we find the following formula (we don’t write the irrelevant numerical constants) :

(5.67)

\displaystyle N^{(n)}\left[\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\right)^{2},\nabla\right]h

\displaystyle=\left(e_{0}^{(n)}\left(\frac{\nabla\beta^{(n)}}{N^{(n)}}\right)+\frac{\left(\nabla\beta^{(n)}\right)^{2}}{N^{(n)}}\right)\nabla h+\left(\frac{\nabla\beta^{(n)}\nabla N^{(n)}}{N^{(n)}}+e_{0}^{(n)}\left(\frac{\nabla N^{(n)}}{N^{(n)}}\right)\right)\mathbf{T}^{(n)}h

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+2\nabla\beta^{(n)}\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\nabla h-2\nabla N^{(n)}\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\right)^{2}h.

We recall that
\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\right)^{2}h=e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}\Delta h+f
, so that the
\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\right)^{2}
term in (
5.67
) has already been estimate during the two first steps. The coefficients in front of
\nabla h
and
\mathbf{T}^{(n)}h
are all in
\mathcal{C}^{1}_{0}
except the two involving
e_{0}\nabla N^{(n)}
, for wich we use the product law
H^{1}\times H^{1}
and (
5.9
) :

\displaystyle\left\|e_{0}^{(n)}\left(\frac{\nabla N^{(n)}}{N^{(n)}}\right)\mathbf{T}^{(n)}h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla\partial_{t}N^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta+1}}\left\|\mathbf{T}^{(n)}h\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\lesssim C(C_{i})\mathcal{E}^{(n)}[h].

We only need to bound the coefficient in front of
\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\nabla
in
L^{\infty}
, which is easily thanks to (
5.10
). This allows us to handle the case
|\alpha|=1
:

\displaystyle\left\|N^{(n)}\left[\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\right)^{2},\nabla^{\alpha}\right]h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}

(5.68)

\displaystyle\leq C(C_{i})\left(\sum_{|\alpha^{\prime}|\leq 1}\left(\left\|\frac{e_{0}^{(n)}\nabla^{\alpha^{\prime}}h}{N^{(n)}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1+|\alpha^{\prime}|}}+\left\|e^{-\gamma^{(n)}}\nabla(\nabla^{\alpha^{\prime}}h)\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1+|\alpha^{\prime}|}}\right)+\|fN^{(n)}\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\right).

Before turning to the case
|\alpha|=2
, let’s remark that, in view of (
5.64
), so far we have proved that

(5.69)

\left\|\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\right)^{2}\nabla h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}\leq C(C_{i})\left(\mathcal{E}^{(n)}[h]+\|fN^{(n)}\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\right).

This means that, even if
\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\right)^{2}\nabla h
doesn’t appear in the expression of
\mathcal{E}^{(n)}[h]
, we are allowed to use it in the sequel of the third step.

We now turn to the case
|\alpha|=2
and push our calculations further to get (we still don’t write the irrelevant numerical constants) :

\displaystyle N^{(n)}\left[\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\right)^{2},\nabla^{2}\right]h=\nabla N^{(n)}\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\right)^{2}\nabla h+\nabla N^{(n)}\left[\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\right)^{2},\nabla\right]h

\displaystyle\left(N^{(n)}\nabla\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\left(\frac{\nabla\beta^{(n)}}{N^{(n)}}\right)+N^{(n)}\nabla\left(\left(\frac{\nabla\beta^{(n)}}{N^{(n)}}\right)^{2}\right)+\frac{\nabla N^{(n)}\left(\nabla\beta^{(n)}\right)^{2}}{N^{(n)}}+\nabla\beta^{(n)}\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\left(\frac{\nabla N^{(n)}}{N^{(n)}}\right)\right)\nabla h

\displaystyle+\left(e_{0}^{(n)}\left(\frac{\nabla\beta^{(n)}}{N^{(n)}}\right)+\frac{\left(\nabla\beta^{(n)}\right)^{2}}{N^{(n)}}\right)\nabla^{2}h+N^{(n)}\nabla\left(\frac{\nabla N^{(n)}}{N^{(n)}}\right)\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\right)^{2}h+\nabla\beta^{(n)}\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\nabla^{2}h

\displaystyle+\left(N^{(n)}\nabla\left(\frac{\nabla\beta^{(n)}\nabla N^{(n)}}{N^{(n)}}\right)+N^{(n)}\nabla\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\left(\frac{\nabla N^{(n)}}{N^{(n)}}\right)+\frac{\nabla\beta^{(n)}\left(\nabla N^{(n)}\right)^{2}}{N^{(n)}}+\nabla N^{(n)}\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\left(\frac{\nabla N^{(n)}}{N^{(n)}}\right)\right)\mathbf{T}^{(n)}h

(5.70)

\displaystyle+\left(\nabla\beta^{(n)}\nabla N^{(n)}+e_{0}^{(n)}\left(\frac{\nabla N^{(n)}}{N^{(n)}}\right)+N^{(n)}\nabla\left(\frac{\nabla\beta^{(n)}}{N^{(n)}}\right)+\frac{\nabla\beta^{(n)}\nabla N^{(n)}}{N^{(n)}}\right)\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\nabla h.

We need to estimate the
L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+3}
norm of (
5.70
). The term
\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\right)^{2}h
has already been handled since
h
satisfies (
5.59
). Since
\nabla N^{(n)}\in C^{0}_{1}
we can use
(
5.69
)
to estimate the term
\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\right)^{2}\nabla h
. With the same argument, using (
5.68
) we handle the
\left[\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\right)^{2},\nabla\right]h
term. Thanks to (
5.12
) and (
5.14
), the coefficients in front of
\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\nabla^{2}h
and
\nabla^{2}h
are in the appropriate weighted
L^{\infty}
-based spaces (
L^{\infty}
and
C^{0}_{1}
respectively). The only problematic terms are the ones where two spatial derivatives hit
\beta^{(n)}
or when at least one spatial derivative and
\mathbf{T}^{(n)}
hit
N^{(n)}
. For them, we use the product estimate (see Proposition
B.2
). Let us give two examples, the first one using the embedding
H^{1}\times H^{1}\xhookrightarrow{}L^{2}
(with appropriate weights) :

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{2}\beta^{(n)}\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\nabla h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla^{2}\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}\left\|\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\nabla h\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}\lesssim C(C_{i})\mathcal{E}^{(n)}[h].

The second example uses the embedding
L^{2}\times H^{2}\xhookrightarrow{}L^{2}
(with appropriate weights) :

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\nabla N^{(n)}\mathbf{T}^{(n)}h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+3}}\lesssim\left(1+\left\|\nabla^{2}\partial_{t}\tilde{N}^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta+2}}\right)\left\|\mathbf{T}^{(n)}h\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\lesssim C(C_{i})\mathcal{E}^{(n)}[h].

This allows us to handle entirely the case
|\alpha|=2
. Summarising the third step, we get :

(5.71)

\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 2}\left\|N^{(n)}\left[\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\right)^{2},\nabla^{\alpha}\right]h\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1+|\alpha|}}\leq C(C_{i})\left(\mathcal{E}^{(n)}[h]+\|fN^{(n)}\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\right).

Combining (
5.65
), (
5.66
) and (
5.71
), we get for all
t\in[0,T]
:

(5.72)

\mathcal{E}^{(n)}[h](t)\leq 2\mathcal{E}^{(n)}[h](0)+C(C_{i})\sqrt{T}\left(\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|fN^{(n)}\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}(s)+\mathcal{E}^{(n)}[h](t)\right).

By choosing
T
sufficiently small, we can absorb the term
\mathcal{E}^{(n)}[h](t)
of the RHS into the LHS and conclude the proof of the lemma.
∎

With this energy estimate, we are ready to prove estimates on
\tilde{\gamma}^{(n+1)}
,
\varphi^{(n+1)}
and
\omega^{(n+1)}
. The spatial term in the energy
\mathcal{E}^{(n)}[h]
is different from what appear in (
5.23
), but (
5.30
) implies that
1\lesssim e^{-\gamma^{(n)}}
so we will get back the estimates we want for
\tilde{\gamma}^{(n+1)}
,
\varphi^{(n+1)}
and
\omega^{(n+1)}
if we bound
\mathcal{E}^{(n)}
, using Lemma
5.4
.

5.2.5.
Hyperbolic estimates

We use our energy estimate to prove the estimates on
\tilde{\gamma}^{(n+1)}
. Since we are not getting
\gamma^{(n+1)}
from an elliptic equation, we cannot obtain the decomposition
\gamma^{(n+1)}=-\alpha\chi\ln+\tilde{\gamma}^{(n+1)}
directly. Our strategy is to solve for
\gamma^{(n+1)}+\alpha\chi\ln
to artificially recover our decomposition after having set
\tilde{\gamma}^{(n+1)}\vcentcolon=\gamma^{(n+1)}+\alpha\chi\ln
. For the sake of clarity, we gather in the following lemma the estimates of the extra terms due to
\alpha\chi\ln
:

Lemma 5.5
.

We set
\Psi^{(n)}=N^{(n)}\left(\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\right)^{2}(\chi\ln)-e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}\Delta(\chi\ln)\right)
.
For
n\geq 2
, the following estimate hold :

(5.73)

\displaystyle\left\|\Psi^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\leq C(C_{i}),

(5.74)

\displaystyle\left\|\Psi^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\leq A_{0}C_{i}.

Proof.

To prove (
5.73
), we don’t need to be very precise about the dependence on
C_{i}
of the bound, so we don’t give many details. For the first part of
\Psi^{(n)}
:

\displaystyle\left\|N^{(n)}\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\right)^{2}(\chi\ln)\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\partial_{t}\left(\frac{\beta^{(n)}}{N^{(n)}}\right)\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}}}+\left\|\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}}}\left(\left\|\frac{\beta^{(n)}}{N^{(n)}}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}-1}}+\left\|\nabla\left(\frac{\beta^{(n)}}{N^{(n)}}\right)\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(C_{i}).

For the second part of
\Psi^{(n)}
, we notice that
\Delta(\chi\ln)=\Delta(\chi)\ln+\nabla\chi\cdot\nabla\ln
is a smooth compactly supported function (its support is included in
B_{2}
) and in particular belongs to all
C^{k}
spaces, using (
4.1
) and (
5.8
) :

\displaystyle\left\|e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}N^{(n)}\Delta(\chi\ln)\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\Delta(\chi\ln)\right\|_{C^{2}}\left\|N^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}(B_{2})}\lesssim 1.

We now turn to the proof of (
5.74
). For the first part of
\Psi^{(n)}
, we use (
5.11
) (and the product estimate
H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}}\times H^{1}_{\eta}\xhookrightarrow{}H^{1}_{\eta}
), (
5.8
), (
5.9
) and (
5.14
), and actually the only term that will bring some
C_{i}
are
\partial_{t}\beta^{(n)}
and
\partial_{t}N^{(n)}
:

\displaystyle\left\|N^{(n)}\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\right)^{2}(\chi\ln)\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\partial_{t}\left(\frac{\beta^{(n)}}{N^{(n)}}\right)\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}}}+\left\|\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}}}\left(\left\|\frac{\beta^{(n)}}{N^{(n)}}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}-1}}+\left\|\nabla\left(\frac{\beta^{(n)}}{N^{(n)}}\right)\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim A_{0}C_{i}+\varepsilon.

For the second part of
\Psi^{(n)}
, we again use the properties of
\Delta(\chi\ln)
, (
4.1
) and (
5.8
) :

\displaystyle\left\|e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}N^{(n)}\Delta(\chi\ln)\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\Delta(\chi\ln)\right\|_{C^{1}}\left\|N^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}(B_{2})}\lesssim 1.

∎

Proposition 5.7
.

For
n\geq 2
the following estimates hold :

(5.75)

\displaystyle\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 2}\left\|\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\nabla^{\alpha}\tilde{\gamma}^{(n+1)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1+|\alpha|}}+\left\|\nabla\tilde{\gamma}^{(n+1)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\leq 8C_{i},

(5.76)

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{t}\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\tilde{\gamma}^{(n+1)}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{i},

(5.77)

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{t}\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\tilde{\gamma}^{(n+1)}\right)\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim A_{1}C_{i}.

Proof.

The strategy is to recover the decomposition of
\gamma^{(n+1)}
by setting
\tilde{\gamma}^{(n+1)}\vcentcolon=\gamma^{(n+1)}+\alpha\chi\ln
. In view of (
5.5
),
\tilde{\gamma}^{(n+1)}
is solution of

(5.78)

\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\right)^{2}\tilde{\gamma}^{(n+1)}-e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}\Delta\tilde{\gamma}^{(n+1)}=\left(\text{RHS of \eqref{reduced system gamma}}\right)+\alpha\frac{\Psi^{(n)}}{N^{(n)}}.

In order to prove (
5.75
) and in view of (
5.63
), we have to bound
\left\|\text{RHS of \eqref{eq gamma tilde}}\times N^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}
, and thanks to the factor
\sqrt{T}
, we don’t need to worry about the bounds. Thanks to (
5.73
), it remains to deal with the RHS of (
5.5
) mutltiplied by
N^{(n)}
, which gives the following expression :

\displaystyle-\frac{N^{(n)}\left(\tau^{(n)}\right)^{2}}{2}+\frac{1}{2}e_{0}^{(n-1)}\left(\frac{\mathrm{div}(\beta^{(n)})}{N^{(n-1)}}\right)

\displaystyle+e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}\frac{\Delta N^{(n)}}{2}+e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}N^{(n)}|\nabla\varphi^{(n)}|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}-4\varphi^{(n)}}N^{(n)}|\nabla\omega^{(n)}|^{2}=\vcentcolon I+II+III+IV+V.

•

For
I
, we mainly use the fact that
H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}
is an algebra and (
5.20
) (and the product estimate to deal with
\chi\ln
) :

\|I\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\lesssim\left\|\tau^{(n)}\right\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\left(1+\left\|\tilde{N}^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\right)\lesssim C(C_{i}).

•

For
II
, we use the computations already performed about
\left[\mathbf{T}^{(n-1)},\nabla\right]
, (
5.9
) (which implies that
\left|\frac{1}{N^{(n-1)}}\right|
and
\left\|\nabla N^{(n-1)}\right\|_{C^{1}}
are bounded) to get rid of the
\frac{1}{N^{(n-1)}}
factors, in order to get :

(5.79)

\displaystyle\|II\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|e_{0}^{(n-1)}N^{(n-1)}\nabla\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}+\left\|\mathbf{T}^{(n-1)}\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{3}_{\delta^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\|\nabla N^{(n-1)}\mathbf{T}^{(n-1)}\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}+\left\|\nabla\beta^{(n-1)}\nabla\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}.

Using (
5.15
), it’s easy to see that
\left\|\mathbf{T}^{(n-1)}\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{3}_{\delta^{\prime}}}\leq C(C_{i})
, and we recall the embedding
H^{3}_{\delta^{\prime}}\xhookrightarrow{}H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}
. Using
|\nabla(\chi\ln)|\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{-1}
and (
5.9
), we see that
\left\|\nabla N^{(n-1)}\right\|_{H^{3}_{\delta}}\lesssim C_{i}
and thus we use the product estimate to write :

\left\|\mathbf{T}^{(n-1)}\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{3}_{\delta^{\prime}}}+\left\|\nabla N^{(n-1)}\mathbf{T}^{(n-1)}\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\lesssim\left\|\mathbf{T}^{(n-1)}\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{3}_{\delta^{\prime}}}\left(1+\left\|\nabla N^{(n-1)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta}}\right)\leq C(C_{i}).

Using
\nabla\beta^{(n)}\in H^{3}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}\xhookrightarrow{}H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}
,
(
5.9
)
, and
\nabla N^{(n-1)}\in H^{2}_{\delta}
:

\displaystyle\left\|e_{0}^{(n-1)}N^{(n-1)}\nabla\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\partial_{t}N_{a}^{(n-1)}\right|\left\|\chi\ln\nabla\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}+\left\|\partial_{t}\tilde{N}^{(n-1)}\nabla\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\|\beta^{(n-1)}\nabla N^{(n-1)}\nabla\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\left|\partial_{t}N_{a}^{(n-1)}\right|+\left\|\partial_{t}\tilde{N}^{(n-1)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta}}\right)\left\|\nabla\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\|\beta^{(n-1)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}\left\|\nabla N^{(n-1)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta}}\left\|\nabla\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(C_{i}).

The last term in (
5.79
) doesn’t present any difficulty and we get
\|II\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\leq C(C_{i})
.

•

For
III
, we first notice that
\Delta(\chi\ln)=\Delta(\chi)\ln+\nabla\chi\cdot\nabla\ln
is a smooth compactly supported function and therefore belongs to all
H^{k}
spaces.
We then use (
4.1
) and (
5.10
) :

\displaystyle\|III\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|N_{a}^{(n)}\right|\left\|\Delta(\chi\ln)\right\|_{H^{2}}+\left\|\Delta\tilde{N}^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta+1}}\leq C(C_{i}).

•

For
IV
, thanks to the support property of
\varphi^{(n)}
, we don’t worry about the decrease of our functions. We simply use (
4.1
), (
5.10
) (which implies that
\left\|N^{(n)}\right\|_{C^{2}(B_{2R})}\leq C(C_{i})
) and the fact that
H^{2}
is an algebra :

\displaystyle\|IV\|_{H^{2}}\lesssim\left\|N^{(n)}\right\|_{C^{2}(B_{2R})}\left\|\nabla\varphi^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}\leq C(C_{i}).

•

For
V
, we do as for
IV
, using in addition (
4.3
), which implies that it remains to deal with the following term :

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{2}\varphi^{(n)}|\nabla\omega^{(n)}|^{2}\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\left\|\nabla^{2}\varphi^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\nabla\omega^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}\leq C(C_{i}).

Using Lemma
5.4
, we get for all
t\in[0,T]
:

\mathcal{E}^{(n)}\left[\tilde{\gamma}^{(n+1)}\right](t)\leq 3\,\mathcal{E}^{(n)}\left[\tilde{\gamma}^{(n+1)}\right](0)+C(C_{i})\sqrt{T}.

It remains to show that
\mathcal{E}^{(n)}\left[\tilde{\gamma}^{(n+1)}\right](0)
is bounded by
C_{i}
. Therefore, the following calculations will be performed on
\Sigma_{0}
and we can forget about the indices
(n)
or
(n+1)
and use the estimates (
4.15
) and (
4.16
), which are more comfortable.
Using the calculations performed in Proposition
5.1
, we show that :

(5.80)

\displaystyle\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 2}\left\|\mathbf{T}\nabla^{\alpha}\tilde{\gamma}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1+|\alpha|}}\leq(1+C\varepsilon)\|\mathbf{T}\tilde{\gamma}\|_{H^{2}_{\delta+1}}+C\varepsilon\mathcal{E}\left[\tilde{\gamma}\right](0)+C\varepsilon\|\tilde{\gamma}\|_{H^{4}_{\delta}}.

Using the same ideas as in the second step of the proof of Lemma
5.4
, we show that :

(5.81)

\displaystyle\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 2}\left\|e^{-\gamma}\nabla(\nabla^{\alpha}\tilde{\gamma})\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1+|\alpha|}}\leq(1+C\varepsilon)\|\tilde{\gamma}\|_{H^{4}_{\delta}}.

Putting together (
5.80
) and (
5.81
) and using (
4.15
) and (
4.16
), we get

\mathcal{E}\left[\tilde{\gamma}\right](0)\leq(1+C\varepsilon)\left(\|\mathbf{T}\tilde{\gamma}\|_{H^{2}_{\delta+1}}+\|\tilde{\gamma}\|_{H^{4}_{\delta}}\right)+C\varepsilon\mathcal{E}\left[\tilde{\gamma}\right](0)\leq 2(1+C\varepsilon)C_{i}+C\varepsilon\mathcal{E}\left[\tilde{\gamma}\right](0).

We can absorb the last term of the RHS into the LHS by choosing
\varepsilon
small enough. Taking
T
small enough and remembering that
1\lesssim e^{-\gamma^{(n)}}
, we finish the proof of (
5.75
).

We now turn to the proof of (
5.76
) and (
5.77
) which amounts to estimating
\partial_{t}\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\tilde{\gamma}^{(n+1)}\right)
, which, thanks to (
5.78
), has the following expression :

(5.82)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\tilde{\gamma}^{(n+1)}\right)

\displaystyle=e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}N^{(n)}\Delta\tilde{\gamma}^{(n+1)}-\frac{N^{(n)}\left(\tau^{(n)}\right)^{2}}{2}+\frac{1}{2}e_{0}^{(n-1)}\left(\frac{\mathrm{div}(\beta^{(n)})}{N^{(n-1)}}\right)

(5.83)

\displaystyle\qquad+e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}\frac{\Delta N^{(n)}}{2}+e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}N^{(n)}\left|\nabla\varphi^{(n)}\right|^{2}

(5.84)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{4}e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}-4\varphi^{(n)}}N^{(n)}|\nabla\omega^{(n)}|^{2}+\alpha\Psi^{(n)}

(5.85)

\displaystyle=\vcentcolon I+II+III+IV+V+VI+VII.

The term
VII
is handled thanks to (
5.74
). For the remainings terms, we first bound their
L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}
norms with
C_{i}
, and then the
H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}
norms of their derivatives by
C_{i}^{2}
.

•

For
I
, we first perform the
H^{1}
estimate, using (
4.1
),
\varepsilon|\chi\ln|\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{\varepsilon}
, (
5.8
) and (
5.75
) :

\displaystyle\|I\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|N^{(n)}\Delta\tilde{\gamma}^{(n+1)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\lesssim\left\|\Delta\tilde{\gamma}^{(n+1)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+2}}\left(1+\left\|\tilde{N}^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta}}\right)\lesssim C_{i}.

To get the
L^{2}
estimate, we simply use the embeddings
H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}\xhookrightarrow{}L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}
.

•

For
II
, we first use (
5.8
) and (
5.32
) :

\|II\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\lesssim\left\|\tau^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}^{2}\left(1+\left\|\tilde{N}^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta}}\right)\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}.

For the
H^{1}
estimate, we use (
5.8
), (
5.32
) and (
5.20
) :

\|II\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\lesssim\left\|\tau^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\left\|\tau^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\left(1+\left\|\tilde{N}^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta}}\right)\lesssim\varepsilon A_{1}C_{i}.

•

For
III
, we use (
5.13
), (
5.11
) and (
5.9
) :

\displaystyle\left\|III\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla\partial_{t}\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}+\left\|\nabla\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\left\|\partial_{t}\tilde{N}^{(n-1)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta}}+\left\|\beta^{(n-1)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}}}\left\|\nabla\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{i}.

For the
H^{1}
estimate, we use :

\displaystyle\left\|III\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla\partial_{t}\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}+\left\|\nabla\beta^{(n)}\partial_{t}\tilde{N}^{(n-1)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}+\left\|\beta^{(n-1)}\nabla\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla\partial_{t}\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}+\left\|\nabla\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\left\|\partial_{t}\tilde{N}^{(n-1)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta}}+\left\|\beta^{(n-1)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}}}\left\|\nabla\beta^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim A_{1}C_{i}+\varepsilon C_{i}+\varepsilon^{2}.

•

For
IV
, we recall that
\Delta(\chi\ln)
is a smooth compactly supported function. We only perform the
H^{1}
estimate, because the
L^{2}
estimate will be a consequence of the embedding
H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}\xhookrightarrow{}L^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}
. We use (
4.1
) and (
5.9
) :

\|IV\|_{H^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}+1}}\lesssim\left|N_{a}^{(n)}\right|\left\|\Delta(\chi\ln)\right\|_{H^{1}}+\left\|\Delta\tilde{N}^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}_{\delta+1}}\lesssim C_{i}.

•

For
V
, we don’t care about the decrease of our functions, thanks to the support property of
\varphi^{(n)}
. We first use (
4.1
) and the fact that
N^{(n)}\in L^{\infty}(B_{2R})
(which comes from (
5.8
)), the Hölder’s inequality and (
5.28
) :

\|V\|_{L^{2}}\leq\left\|N^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{2R})}\left\|\nabla\varphi^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}.

For the
H^{1}
estimate, we use (
4.1
), (
5.8
), (
5.9
) and (
5.26
) :

\displaystyle\|\nabla V\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla\varphi^{(n)}\nabla^{2}\varphi^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\tilde{N}^{(n)}\left|\nabla\varphi^{(n)}\right|^{2}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla^{2}\varphi^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{1}}\left\|\nabla\varphi^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{4}}+\left\|\nabla\tilde{N}^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}_{\delta+1}}\left\|\nabla\varphi^{(n)}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon A_{0}C_{i}.

•

For
VI
, we do as for
V
, using in addition (
4.3
).

∎

We are now interesting in proving estimates for
\varphi^{(n+1)}
and
\omega^{(n+1)}
. We first prove their support property :

Lemma 5.6
.

There exists
C_{s}>0
such that for
\varepsilon
,
T
sufficiently small (depending on
R
),
\varphi^{(n+1)}
is supported in

\left\{(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ |x|\leq R+C_{s}(1+R^{\varepsilon})t\right\}.

In particular, choosing
T
small enough,
\mathrm{supp}(\varphi^{(n+1)})\subset[0,T]\times B_{2R}
.

Proof.

Since the initial data for
\varphi^{(n+1)}
and
\partial_{t}\varphi^{(n+1)}
are compactly supported and
\Box_{g^{(n)}}\varphi^{(n+1)}
is compactly supported in

A\vcentcolon=\left\{(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ |x|\leq R+C_{s}(1+R^{\varepsilon})t\right\}

we juste have to show that
\partial A
is a spacelike hypersurface. We set
f(x,t)=-|x|+C_{s}(1+R^{\varepsilon})t
, in order to have
\partial A=f^{-1}(-R)
. Thus, we have to show that
(g^{(n)})^{-1}(\mathrm{d}f,\mathrm{d}f)
is non-positive on this hypersurface. We have
\mathrm{d}f=-\frac{x_{i}}{|x|}\mathrm{d}x^{i}+C_{s}(1+R^{\varepsilon})\mathrm{d}t
, which implies :

\displaystyle\left(g^{(n)}\right)^{-1}(\mathrm{d}f,\mathrm{d}f)

\displaystyle=\frac{x_{i}x_{j}}{|x|^{2}}\left(g^{(n)}\right)^{ij}+C_{s}^{2}(1+R^{\varepsilon})^{2}\left(g^{(n)}\right)^{tt}-\frac{2x_{i}}{|x|}C_{s}(1+R^{\varepsilon})\left(g^{(n)}\right)^{it}

\displaystyle=e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}-\left(\frac{x\cdot\beta^{(n)}}{|x|N^{(n)}}\right)^{2}-\left(\frac{C_{s}(1+R^{\varepsilon})}{N^{(n)}}\right)^{2}-\frac{2(x\cdot\beta^{(n)})C_{s}(1+R^{\varepsilon})}{(N^{(n)})^{2}|x|}

We have
e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{2\varepsilon^{2}}
,
\frac{|x\cdot\beta^{(n)}|}{(N^{(n)})^{2}|x|}+\left(\frac{x\cdot\beta^{(n)}}{|x|N^{(n)}}\right)^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon
, so choosing the parameters appropriately, one easily sees that
(g^{(n)})^{-1}(\mathrm{d}f,\mathrm{d}f)
is non-positive on the hypersurface.
∎

Proposition 5.8
.

For
n\geq 2
, the following estimates holds :

(5.86)

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{t}\varphi^{(n+1)}\right\|_{H^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\varphi^{(n+1)}\right\|_{H^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{i},

(5.87)

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{t}\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\varphi^{(n+1)}\right)\right\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{i}.

Proof.

First, note that since
\varphi^{(n+1)}
is compactly supported in
B_{2R}
for all time by previous lemma, we do not need to worry about the spatial decay in this proof. We recall the wave equation satisfied by
\varphi^{(n+1)}
:

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\right)^{2}\varphi^{(n+1)}-e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}\Delta\varphi^{(n+1)}

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}}{N^{(n)}}\nabla\varphi^{(n)}\cdot\nabla N^{(n)}+\frac{\tau^{(n)}e_{0}^{(n-1)}\varphi^{(n)}}{N^{(n)}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2}e^{-4\varphi^{(n)}}\left(\left(e_{0}^{(n-1)}\omega^{(n)}\right)^{2}+\left|\nabla\omega^{(n)}\right|^{2}\right).

Using our energy estimate for this wave equation we see that to prove the first part of the proposition we have to bound

\left\|e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}\nabla\varphi^{(n)}\cdot\nabla N^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}}+\left\|\tau^{(n)}e_{0}^{(n-1)}\varphi^{(n)}\right\|_{H^{2}}+\left\|e^{-4\varphi^{(n)}}N^{(n)}(e_{0}^{(n-1)}\omega^{(n)})^{2}\right\|_{H^{2}}+\left\|e^{-4\varphi^{(n)}}N^{(n)}|\nabla\omega^{(n)}|^{2}\right\|_{H^{2}}.

We mainly use the fact that in dimension 2,
H^{2}
is an algebra. Noting that every norm is not taking on the whole space but only on
B_{2R}
, using (
4.1
) and (
4.3
) and thanks to the estimates made on the
n
-th iterate, it’s easy to see that this quantity is bounded by some constant
C(A_{i},C_{i})
. We also recall that :

\mathcal{E}^{(n)}\left[\varphi^{(n+1)}\right](0)\lesssim C_{i}.

Thanks to the Lemma
5.4
, if
T
is small enough, we have for all
t\in[0,T]

\mathcal{E}^{(n)}\left[\varphi^{(n+1)}\right](t)\lesssim C_{i}.

Thanks to the support property of
\varphi^{(n+1)}
, the fact that
1\lesssim e^{-\gamma^{(n)}}
and
|N^{(n)}|\lesssim 1
(on
B_{2R}
), we have :

(5.88)

\left\|\partial_{t}\varphi^{(n+1)}\right\|_{H^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\varphi^{(n+1)}\right\|_{H^{2}}\lesssim\mathcal{E}^{(n)}\left[\varphi^{(n+1)}\right]+C_{i}.

which concludes the proof of (
5.86
).

We next prove the estimate (
5.87
). We use the equation satisfied by
\varphi^{(n+1)}
to express the term we want to estimate:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\varphi^{(n+1)}\right)

\displaystyle=e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}N^{(n)}\Delta\varphi^{(n+1)}+e^{-2\gamma^{(n)}}\nabla\varphi^{(n)}\cdot\nabla N^{(n)}+\tau^{(n)}e_{0}^{(n-1)}\varphi^{(n)}+\beta^{(n)}\cdot\nabla\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\varphi^{(n+1)}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2}e^{-4\varphi^{(n)}}N^{(n)}\left(e_{0}^{(n-1)}\omega^{(n)}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}e^{-4\varphi^{(n)}}N^{(n)}\left|\nabla\omega^{(n)}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\vcentcolon I+II+III+IV+V+VI.

Thus, it remains to bound those terms by
C_{i}
in
H^{1}
, and the main difficulty is avoiding any
C_{i}^{2}
bound. We mainly use the embedding of
H^{1}
in
L^{q}
for all
q\geq 2
and the Hölder inequality, in particular the
L^{4}\times L^{4}\xhookrightarrow{}L^{2}
and
L^{8}\times L^{8}\xhookrightarrow{}L^{4}
case (note that in the following we do not write down the factors that are trivially in
L^{\infty}
) :

•

for
I
, the only issues are the terms where
\tilde{N}^{(n)}
or
\tilde{\gamma}^{(n)}
get one derivative :

\displaystyle\|I\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\varphi^{(n+1)}\|_{H^{2}}+\|\nabla\tilde{N}^{(n)}\Delta\varphi^{(n+1)}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\tilde{\gamma}^{(n)}\Delta\varphi^{(n+1)}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\varphi^{(n+1)}\|_{H^{2}}\left(1+\|\nabla\tilde{N}^{(n)}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\nabla\tilde{\gamma}^{(n)}\|_{H^{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{i}.

•

for
II
, we forget about the
\chi\ln
in
N^{(n)}
, which is less problematic than
\tilde{N}^{(n)}
:

\displaystyle\|II\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\varphi^{(n)}\cdot\nabla N^{(n)}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla^{2}\varphi^{(n)}\nabla N^{(n)}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\varphi^{(n)}\nabla^{2}N^{(n)}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\tilde{\gamma}^{(n)}\nabla\varphi^{(n)}\nabla N^{(n)}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\varphi^{(n)}\|_{L^{4}}\|\tilde{N}^{(n)}\|_{H^{4}}+\|\nabla\varphi^{(n)}\|_{H^{2}}\|\tilde{N}^{(n)}\|_{H^{2}}\left(1+\|\tilde{\gamma}^{(n)}\|_{H^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{i}.

•

for
III
, we use (
5.20
) when no derivatives hits
e_{0}^{(n-1)}\varphi^{(n)}
and (
5.32
) when one derivative hits
e_{0}^{(n-1)}\varphi^{(n)}
:

\displaystyle\|III\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tau^{(n)}\|_{H^{2}}\left(\|\partial_{t}\varphi^{(n)}\|_{L^{4}}+\|\nabla\varphi^{(n)}\|_{L^{4}}\left(1+\left\|\nabla\beta^{(n-1)}\right\|_{H^{1}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\|\tau^{(n)}\|_{H^{1}}\left(\|\nabla^{2}\varphi^{(n)}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\partial_{t}\nabla\varphi^{(n)}\|_{H^{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{i}.

•

for
IV
, we just notice that, applying the same type of arguments as in Proposition
5.1
, it’s easy to deduce from the first part of this proof that
\left\|\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\varphi^{(n+1)}\right\|_{H^{2}}\lesssim C_{i}
:

\displaystyle\|IV\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\varphi^{(n+1)}\right\|_{H^{1}}+\left\|\nabla\beta^{(n)}\nabla\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\varphi^{(n+1)}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\beta^{(n)}\nabla^{2}\left(\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\varphi^{(n+1)}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\mathbf{T}^{(n)}\varphi^{(n+1)}\right\|_{H^{2}}\left(1+\|\nabla\beta^{(n)}\|_{H^{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{i}.

•

for
V
, we use first (
4.3
) and the fact that
N^{(n)}
and
\nabla N^{(n)}
are bounded, and then (
5.28
) and (
5.27
) :

\displaystyle\left\|V\right\|_{H^{1}}
